غذائی و مشروبات سازی کی صنعت کا تعلیمی متن
Food and Beverage Manufacturing Industry Skill Measurement Test Study Text
Version 5.0 Specified Skilled Worker No. 1
Organization: General Incorporated Association Organization for Technical Skill Assessment of Foreign Workers in Food Industry
December 2024
تعارف
Introduction
This “Food and Beverage Manufacturing Industry Skill Measurement Test Study Text” is a study text for people who want to work in the food and beverage manufacturing industry, such as food factories, under the Specified Skilled Worker No. 1 status.
Note: “Food and beverage” means drinks and food products.
The structure of the previous text has been organized and revised so that learners can study in a more systematic and practical way.
This text introduces not only the knowledge and skills needed for work, but also basic terms based on the fundamental ideas of food and beverage manufacturing.
Depending on the workplace, the methods and terms used may be different from this text. In that case, please follow the rules and methods of your workplace. Even if the method or terms are different, the basic idea is the same.
Please use this text to study the knowledge and skills of food hygiene and occupational safety, which are necessary for Specified Skilled Worker No. 1.

باب 1: غذائی و مشروبات سازی کی صنعت میں ضروری مہارتیں
Chapter 1: Skills Required in the Food and Beverage Manufacturing Industry
1. مختلف غذائی و مشروبات سازی کی صنعتیں
1. Various Food and Beverage Manufacturing Industries
Drinks and food products are essential in our daily lives. The work of manufacturing drinks and food products in factories is called the food and beverage manufacturing industry.
There are many types of food and beverage manufacturing industries, such as:
Ham and sausage manufacturing
Canned fish manufacturing
Seasoning manufacturing
Bread and confectionery manufacturing
Bento lunch box and prepared food manufacturing
Frozen food manufacturing
Juice, tea, and coffee drink manufacturing
According to statistics from the Ministry of Agriculture, Forestry and Fisheries, the domestic production value of Japan’s food manufacturing industry has been around 36 to 38 trillion yen in recent years.
The chart on page 2 shows the movement of domestic production value in Japan’s food manufacturing industry. It shows that in 2022, the estimated domestic production value was 38.4 trillion yen.
2022 Estimated Breakdown
Beverages: 4.0 trillion yen, 10%
Seasonings: 1.7 trillion yen, 5%
Retort foods: 0.2 trillion yen, 1%
Frozen prepared foods: 0.7 trillion yen, 2%
Confectionery: 3.4 trillion yen, 9%
Bread products: 1.9 trillion yen, 5%
Noodles: 1.0 trillion yen, 3%
Grain milling and flour products: 2.8 trillion yen, 7%
Marine food products: 3.4 trillion yen, 9%
Livestock food products: 3.6 trillion yen, 9%
Meat: 2.8 trillion yen, 7%
Prepared foods, sushi, and bento: 2.8 trillion yen, 7%
Others: 9.8 trillion yen, 26%
2. مخصوص ہنر مند کارکن نمبر 1 کے لیے ضروری مہارتیں
2. Skills Required for Specified Skilled Worker No. 1 in the Food and Beverage Manufacturing Industry
The types of work where people with the residence status of Specified Skilled Worker No. 1 in the food and beverage manufacturing industry may work include:
Livestock food product manufacturing
Marine food product manufacturing
Seasoning manufacturing
Bread and confectionery manufacturing
Prepared food manufacturing
Frozen prepared food manufacturing
Soft drink manufacturing
In every type of work, knowledge and skills related to food hygiene and occupational safety are necessary.
2.1 فوڈ ہائیجین / غذائی صفائی
2.1 Food Hygiene
“Hygiene” means protecting life.
“Food hygiene” means that when people eat food, they do not become sick and do not get injured.
Producing and managing food so that the people who eat it do not become sick or injured is called hygiene management in food manufacturing.
If people eat food that has not been properly managed for hygiene, meaning unsafe food, they may become sick or injured.
The company that made that food will also lose trust.
2.2 کام کی جگہ کی حفاظت
2.2 Occupational Safety
In food manufacturing factories, workplace accidents such as injuries can occur.
To avoid injuries in factories and work areas, workers must correctly wear safety protective equipment such as:
Work clothes
Helmets
Other safety protective equipment
Workers must also follow the rules and work procedure manuals decided by the factory.
When using machines and tools, workers must follow the factory rules and work procedure manuals.
Protecting the safety and health of workers is called occupational safety.
باب 2: فوڈ ہائیجین / غذائی صفائی
Chapter 2: Food Hygiene
1. فوڈ ہائیجین کیا ہے؟
1. What is Food Hygiene?
“Food hygiene” means that people do not become sick and do not get injured after eating food.
The basics of food hygiene include actions such as:
Washing hands
Wearing work clothes and gloves correctly
Using clean facilities, machines, and tools
Storing food and raw materials at the decided temperature
These basic hygiene controls are common in every workplace and for every type of food. They are called general hygiene management.
In general hygiene management, the following points are important:
5S
Hygiene management of facilities, equipment, and tools
Hygiene management of workers
Hygiene management of raw materials and food products
After general hygiene management is carried out, some workplaces also decide especially important processes according to the type of food, workplace, facilities, and machines.
These especially important processes are called Critical Control Points, or CCP.
The hygiene management system that combines general hygiene management and control of Critical Control Points is called HACCP hygiene management.
2. HACCP کیا ہے؟
2. What is HACCP?
HACCP means:
Hazard Analysis and Critical Control Point
In Japanese, it is translated as:
Hazard Factor Analysis and Critical Control Point
However, it is usually used as HACCP.
A hazard means a cause that can make people sick or injured.
Making food so that people do not become sick or injured means making food so that hazards are not included in it.
Both general hygiene management and HACCP hygiene management are activities to prevent hazards from entering food.
3. خطرے کے عوامل
3. Hazards
There are three types of hazards:
Physical hazards
Chemical hazards
Biological hazards
In food manufacturing, workers must understand these hazards and prevent them from entering food.
3.1 جسمانی خطرے کے عوامل
3.1 Physical Hazards
Physical hazards are things that may physically injure a person.
For example:
Something hard may break a person’s tooth.
Something sharp may injure the inside of a person’s mouth.
Typical physical hazards include:
Metal pieces
Glass pieces
Plastic pieces
Stones
Metal, glass, or plastic pieces may already be mixed into raw materials, or they may enter food from machines during manufacturing.
Stones may also be mixed into raw materials.
Something that should not be in food is called a foreign object.
For example, eggshells that should have been removed but remain in omelets or cooked egg dishes are also foreign objects.
Hard foreign objects such as metal pieces, glass pieces, and stones are called hard foreign objects.
To prevent physical hazards from entering food, workers must prevent hard foreign objects from mixing into food.
For this purpose, workers should:
Wash vegetables that may have soil or small stones on them
Sort raw materials carefully
Remove unnecessary parts during preparation
Cut off the roots of vegetables
Remove shellfish shells
Remove fish gills and bones
Cover prepared ingredients and cooked food during storage
Foreign objects such as insects, hair, and paper pieces are not hard, so they usually do not injure the mouth or throat. Therefore, they are not classified as physical hazards.
However, if such foreign objects are found in food, customers may think the food is dirty and unhygienic. This can lead to complaints and returns.
So, even soft foreign objects such as insects, hair, and paper pieces must not enter food.
If a worker finds any foreign object, whether hard or soft, they must immediately stop work and report it to the person in charge.
Foreign objects are not only hazards. They can also damage the company’s trust.
3.2 کیمیائی خطرے کے عوامل
3.2 Chemical Hazards
Chemical hazards are chemical substances that may cause harm.
Examples include:
Allergens
Histamine
Solanine in potato sprouts
Chemicals such as disinfectants and sanitizers
3.2.1 الرجی پیدا کرنے والے مادے
3.2.1 Allergens
An allergen is a substance that causes an allergic reaction.
An allergic reaction means that the body reacts too strongly to a substance that has entered the body, and harmful symptoms appear.
Symptoms may include:
Itching
Hives
Swelling of lips or eyelids
Runny nose
Sneezing that does not stop
Difficulty breathing
Nausea
If the symptoms are serious, a person may die.
In Japan, the law requires processed foods that use ingredients containing allergens to show those ingredients on the label.
Among these allergens:
Items that must be displayed are called specified raw materials.
Items that are recommended to be displayed are called items equivalent to specified raw materials.
3.2.2 لازمی الرجی لیبل والے اجزاء
3.2.2 Specified Raw Materials — Labeling Required
There are 8 items for which labeling is required:
Shrimp
Crab
Walnut
Wheat
Buckwheat
Egg
Milk
Peanut
3.2.3 الرجی لیبل کے لیے تجویز کردہ اجزاء
3.2.3 Items Equivalent to Specified Raw Materials — Labeling Recommended
There are 20 items for which labeling is recommended:
Almond
Abalone
Squid
Salmon roe
Orange
Cashew nut
Kiwi fruit
Beef
Sesame
Salmon
Mackerel
Soybean
Chicken
Banana
Pork
Macadamia nut
Peach
Yam
Apple
Gelatin
Note: From March 28, 2024, macadamia nut was added to this recommended list, and matsutake mushroom was removed.
Machines and tools used for cooking ingredients that contain allergens must not be used for the next ingredients without cleaning.
This is to prevent allergens from entering food that should not contain allergens.
Machines and tools used for ingredients containing allergens must always be washed.
It is also important to separate cooking tools such as cutting boards and knives by ingredient type.
For example, the cutting board and knife used for boiled eggs should be different from those used for other ingredients. This prevents egg allergens from attaching to other ingredients.
Cleaning agents and disinfectants are also chemical hazards.
When cleaning agents or disinfectants are used on raw materials or cooking tools, they must be washed away thoroughly with water so that they do not remain in food.
3.2.4 ہسٹامین
3.2.4 Histamine
After fish are caught, if they are not refrigerated or frozen immediately and are stored in poor conditions, they may contain a substance called histamine.
This is especially common in red-flesh fish such as:
Tuna
Horse mackerel
Sardine
Mackerel
If a person eats fish or fish dishes containing histamine, symptoms similar to an allergic reaction may occur.
This is called histamine food poisoning.
Histamine increases when storage conditions are poor. Therefore, fish received as raw materials must be refrigerated or frozen immediately.
3.2.5 سولانین
3.2.5 Solanine
Potato sprouts and green potato skin contain a substance called solanine.
If a person eats food containing solanine, food poisoning symptoms may occur, such as:
Nausea
Vomiting
Diarrhea
Stomach pain
Headache
Solanine food poisoning can be prevented by removing potato sprouts and green parts of the potato skin.
3.3 حیاتیاتی خطرے کے عوامل
3.3 Biological Hazards
Biological hazards are living organisms or microorganisms that cause food poisoning.
Examples include:
Bacteria
Viruses
Parasites
Mold
Among biological hazards, bacteria and viruses that cause disease are also called pathogenic microorganisms.
Microorganisms means very small living things, such as bacteria and viruses, that cannot be seen without a microscope.
3.3.1 بیکٹیریا / فوڈ پوائزننگ بیکٹیریا
3.3.1 Bacteria / Food Poisoning Bacteria
Among pathogenic microorganisms, bacteria that cause food poisoning are called food poisoning bacteria.
Main food poisoning bacteria and symptoms
Some bacteria make spores.
Spores are like seeds of bacteria covered with a shell. Normal bacteria die with heat, but some bacteria can make spores and survive when the environment is difficult, such as during heating or drying.
When the environment becomes suitable again, the spores return to normal bacteria and start increasing.
3.3.2 فوڈ پوائزننگ سے بچاؤ کے تین اصول
3.3.2 Three Principles for Preventing Food Poisoning
To prevent food poisoning caused by bacteria, the following three principles are important:
Do not attach bacteria to food
Do not allow bacteria attached to food to increase
Destroy / kill bacteria attached to food
3.3.3 بیکٹیریا کو کھانے پر نہ لگنے دیں
3.3.3 Do Not Attach Bacteria to Food
This means preventing food poisoning bacteria from attaching to clean food and raw materials.
For this purpose:
Wash hands before starting work and whenever necessary
Follow the decided hand-washing method
Wash and sanitize machines
Wash and sanitize cooking tools
3.3.4 بیکٹیریا کو بڑھنے نہ دیں
3.3.4 Do Not Allow Bacteria to Increase
This means preventing food poisoning bacteria from increasing inside food.
Bacteria need the following to increase:
Temperature
Water
Nutrition
Many bacteria prefer:
High temperature: 20°C to 50°C
High humidity or moisture
Bacteria increase especially quickly at 30°C to 40°C.
On the other hand:
Below 10°C, bacteria increase slowly
Below 4°C, bacteria almost do not increase
Below -15°C, bacteria do not increase
Therefore, to prevent food poisoning bacteria from increasing, it is important to store food at low temperature.
Some food poisoning bacteria make toxins. These toxins can also cause food poisoning.
Some toxins made by bacteria such as Staphylococcus aureus and Bacillus cereus do not disappear even after heating.
For this reason, it is especially important not to allow these bacteria to increase, so that they do not produce toxins.
3.3.5 بیکٹیریا کو ختم کریں / مار دیں
3.3.5 Destroy / Kill Bacteria
This means killing food poisoning bacteria attached to raw materials or food.
When food is sterilized by heating, it is important to heat it at the decided temperature for the decided time.
Many food poisoning bacteria can be killed by heating at 75°C or higher for at least 1 minute.
However, stronger conditions may be necessary to kill spores.
3.3.6 وائرس / نورو وائرس
3.3.6 Viruses / Norovirus
A typical virus that causes food poisoning is Norovirus.
Shellfish such as oysters may sometimes be contaminated with Norovirus.
Norovirus does not increase inside food. However, it increases inside the body, especially in the intestines, of an infected person.
It can cause symptoms such as:
Stomach pain
Diarrhea
Vomiting
Norovirus is found in human stool and vomit.
The basic way to prevent food from being contaminated with Norovirus is to wash hands properly.
Handwashing after using the toilet is especially important.
To kill Norovirus by heating, the center temperature of the raw material or food must be heated at:
85°C to 90°C for 90 seconds or more
Norovirus does not die by alcohol disinfection. After washing machines and tools used for cooking, they should be disinfected by soaking them in sodium hypochlorite solution of 200 ppm, or by wiping them with a cloth soaked in sodium hypochlorite solution.
3.3.7 نورو وائرس سے فوڈ پوائزننگ روکنے کے چار اصول
3.3.7 Four Principles for Preventing Norovirus Food Poisoning
Norovirus does not increase inside food. Therefore, among the three principles of preventing food poisoning, the measure “do not allow bacteria to increase” is not effective for Norovirus.
To prevent food poisoning caused by Norovirus, the following ideas are important:
1. فیکٹری میں وائرس نہ لائیں
1. Do not bring Norovirus into the food factory
Before entering the food manufacturing factory, workers must confirm that they are not infected with Norovirus.
Workers must also wash their hands properly.
2. کھانے پر وائرس نہ لگنے دیں
2. Do not attach Norovirus to food
Workers must prevent Norovirus from attaching to food, raw materials, machines, tools, and hands.
Handwashing is very important, especially after using the toilet.
3. وائرس کو ختم کریں
3. Kill Norovirus
Food must be heated properly.
The center temperature should reach 85°C to 90°C for 90 seconds or more.
Machines and tools should be washed and then disinfected with sodium hypochlorite solution.
4. وائرس کو پھیلنے نہ دیں
4. Do not spread Norovirus
If a worker is infected with Norovirus, it is important to disinfect the inside of the food manufacturing factory properly.
Vomit must be handled correctly using special tools. This is called not spreading the virus.
4. عمومی صفائی کا انتظام
4. General Hygiene Management
General hygiene management means the basic hygiene control that is common in all food manufacturing workplaces.
It includes:
Keeping facilities, equipment, and tools clean
Preventing pathogenic microorganisms from entering through workers’ hands and work clothes
Preventing microorganisms from attaching to raw materials and food
Removing hard foreign objects
Following correct rules every day
The basic idea of general hygiene management is 5S.
4.1 فائیو ایس / 5S
4.1 5S
5S means five important actions used to keep the workplace safe, clean, and organized.
The five points are:
Seiri — Sorting
Seiton — Setting in order
Seiso — Cleaning
Seiketsu — Cleanliness / Sanitary condition
Shukan-zuke or Shitsuke — Habit / Discipline
4.1.1 سیری / غیر ضروری چیزیں الگ کرنا
4.1.1 Seiri — Sorting
This means separating necessary things and unnecessary things.
Unnecessary things should be removed from the workplace.
In a food factory, unnecessary items can become a cause of contamination, foreign objects, or accidents.
4.1.2 سیتون / چیزوں کو ترتیب سے رکھنا
4.1.2 Seiton — Setting in Order
This means placing necessary things in a fixed place so that they can be used easily.
Tools, equipment, raw materials, and cleaning items should be kept in their proper places.
This helps workers avoid mistakes and work safely.
4.1.3 سیسو / صفائی کرنا
4.1.3 Seiso — Cleaning
This means removing dirt and keeping the workplace clean.
Workers must clean facilities, machines, tools, floors, and work areas.
Cleaning helps prevent contamination and food poisoning.
4.1.4 سیکیتسو / صاف اور صحت مند حالت برقرار رکھنا
4.1.4 Seiketsu — Cleanliness / Sanitary Condition
This means that after sorting, setting in order, and cleaning, the workplace looks clean and has no food hygiene problems.
It also means that equipment and tools are not contaminated with pathogenic microorganisms.
4.1.5 شیتسوکے / عادت اور نظم و ضبط
4.1.5 Shukan-zuke / Shitsuke — Habit and Discipline
This means being able to do the decided things in the decided way.
Workers must follow the rules of sorting, setting in order, cleaning, and cleanliness every day.
General hygiene management is based on the idea of 5S. It helps keep facilities, equipment, and tools clean, prevents contamination from workers’ hands and clothes, prevents contamination of raw materials and food, and removes hard foreign objects.
4.2 سہولتوں، مشینوں اور آلات کی صفائی کا انتظام
4.2 Hygiene Management of Facilities, Equipment, and Tools
Facilities, equipment, and tools must be managed correctly so that hazards do not enter food.
This means workers must prevent:
Metal pieces from mixing into food
Chemicals from mixing into food
Pathogenic microorganisms from attaching to food
Pathogenic microorganisms from increasing in food
4.2.1 کراس کنٹامینیشن / ایک چیز سے دوسری چیز آلودہ ہونا
4.2.1 Cross Contamination
When clean people or clean things touch unclean people or unclean things, they may become contaminated with hazards.
This is called cross contamination.
Examples of “things” include:
Raw materials
Food products
Machines
Tools
Containers
To prevent cross contamination, it is important to:
Use facilities, equipment, and tools separately according to purpose
Keep facilities, equipment, and tools clean
4.2.2 زوننگ / علاقے الگ کرکے صفائی کا انتظام
4.2.2 Zoning / Divided Hygiene Management
To prevent cross contamination, food factories are usually divided into different areas.
These areas include:
Clean work area
Semi-clean work area
Contaminated work area
Managing hygiene by dividing areas like this is called zoning or divided hygiene management.
4.2.3 صاف کام کا علاقہ
4.2.3 Clean Work Area
In a clean work area, workers handle food after it has been sterilized by heating or chemicals.
For example, in prepared food manufacturing, this area is used for:
Topping
Plating / arranging food
Packaging
This area must be kept especially clean because the food has already been treated and may be eaten soon.
4.2.4 نیم صاف کام کا علاقہ
4.2.4 Semi-Clean Work Area
In a semi-clean work area, workers handle raw materials before sterilization.
Examples include:
Preparation work
Heat cooking
Processing before final cooking or sterilization
In this area, food that is already being processed must not touch raw materials before work starts.
4.2.5 آلودہ کام کا علاقہ
4.2.5 Contaminated Work Area
A contaminated work area is the area where raw materials brought from outside the workplace are handled.
Examples include:
Receiving raw materials
Handling products packed in cardboard boxes for shipment
Products packed in cardboard boxes may also be handled in a contaminated work area.
Workers must follow these rules:
Workers from contaminated areas must not enter semi-clean or clean areas without following the correct procedure.
Raw materials from contaminated or semi-clean areas must not pass through clean areas.
Raw materials must not be brought into semi-clean or clean areas while still inside cardboard boxes or containers.
Cardboard boxes and containers may be contaminated with soil, dust, insects, or microorganisms.
Unneeded raw materials and garbage from cleaning must be taken out through the decided route.
Some factories decide a specific time for taking garbage through the passage.
After using a passage for garbage removal, cleaning, washing, and disinfection may be needed to prevent cross contamination.
4.3 صفائی، دھلائی اور جراثیم کشی
4.3 Cleaning, Washing, and Disinfection
Facilities and equipment must be cleaned regularly and always kept clean.
If cleaning is not done, insects and rats may enter and increase inside the workplace. This makes the hygiene condition of the facilities and equipment worse.
Special care is needed for:
Drainage channels
Areas under large machines
These places can easily become sources of insects.
Drainage channels should be washed after removing their covers.
The floor of the workplace must not be left wet because microorganisms can easily increase in wet places.
4.3.1 مشینوں کی صفائی
4.3.1 Cleaning Machines
Machines such as mixers, slicers, and food cutters must always be washed when raw materials are changed.
They must also be washed after work is finished.
Parts that can be disassembled should be taken apart and washed.
Machines must be washed every day, even if they do not look dirty.
After washing machines carefully, the parts that directly touch food, such as blades, must be disinfected.
4.3.2 آلات کی صفائی
4.3.2 Cleaning Tools
If dirt remains on tools such as knives and cutting boards, microorganisms can increase there.
Water remaining on tools can also cause microorganisms to increase.
After using tools, workers must:
Wash them using the decided method
Dry them so that no water remains
Store them properly
Cleaning tools such as brushes, sponges, mops, cloths, water wipers, and deck brushes must not be left dirty or wet.
If they are left dirty or wet, microorganisms may increase.
4.4 کیمیکلز کا انتظام
4.4 Chemical Management
If chemicals mix into food, the person who eats the food may become sick.
Also, if chemicals get into the eyes or touch the skin of workers, they may cause serious injury, such as loss of eyesight or burns.
Chemicals must be stored correctly in the decided place.
Chemicals such as disinfectants and cleaning agents must not be stored in the same storage area as raw materials.
The name of each chemical must be clearly displayed.
If a worker finds a chemical without a label, they must immediately report it to the person in charge.
5. کارکنوں کی صفائی کا انتظام
5. Hygiene Management of Workers
Workers must not bring into the workplace anything that can become a foreign object or pathogenic microorganism that causes food poisoning.
Workers must also prevent cross contamination from:
Work clothes
Hands and fingers
Shoes
Hats
Gloves
The following points are necessary for worker hygiene management.
5.1 صحت کی جانچ
5.1 Health Check
Before starting work, workers must check that they do not have symptoms such as:
Diarrhea
Fever
Nausea
Cuts or wounds on hands and fingers
This is called a health check.
A worker who has these symptoms must always report to the person in charge.
Even if a worker has no symptoms at the start of work, symptoms may appear during work. For example:
Stomach pain
Injury or cut on hands or fingers
In such cases, the worker must not continue working.
They must stop work immediately and report to the person in charge.
Workers must also take regular health examinations and stool tests decided by the workplace.
5.2 کام کے کپڑے
5.2 Work Clothes
5.2.1 کام کے کپڑے صحیح طریقے سے پہننا
5.2.1 Wearing Work Clothes Correctly
Workers must wear work clothes correctly to prevent:
Dirt entering the workplace
Foreign objects entering the workplace
Microorganisms attaching to raw materials, machines, and tools
Workers must follow these rules:
Wear work clothes in the changing room.
Wear clean work clothes, masks, hairnets, hats, shoes, and gloves that fit your size.
Keep nails short at all times.
Do not enter the workplace with artificial nails or nail polish.
Do not wear rings, bracelets, watches, earrings, necklaces, piercings, hairpins, false eyelashes, or accessories in the workplace.
Do not bring unnecessary items into the workplace.
Workers must check in a mirror that they are wearing work clothes correctly.
For example, check that:
Inner clothes are not coming out from the work clothes.
The nose is not outside the mask.
Ears and hair are not coming out from the cap or hairnet.
5.2.2 کام کے کپڑے پہننے کی عام ترتیب
5.2.2 General Order for Wearing Work Clothes
Work clothes are generally worn in this order:
1. برشنگ
1. Brushing
Brush the hair well.
2. ہیئر نیٹ
2. Hairnet
Wear the hairnet.
Make sure hair does not come out of the hairnet.
3. ٹوپی
3. Cap
Wear the cap properly.
4. کام کے کپڑے
4. Work Clothes
Wear the work clothes.
Fasten buttons and zippers properly.
5. آخری چیک
5. Final Check
Finally, check that the clothing is correct.
5.2.3 کام کے دوران لباس کے اصول
5.2.3 Rules During Work
Workers must wear work clothes correctly at all times during work.
For example:
Do not remove the cap because the workplace is hot.
Do not roll up the sleeves of work clothes.
Do not remove the mask.
Before entering the workplace, remove hair and dust from work clothes using:
Adhesive roller
Air shower
5.2.4 زوننگ اور کام کے کپڑے
5.2.4 Zoning and Work Clothes
Workers must not go outside the workplace while wearing the same work clothes, cap, or shoes used in the workplace.
If they return to the workplace in the same clothes, cap, or shoes, they may bring in:
Soil
Dust
Insects
Microorganisms
In the toilet, workers must change into toilet-use footwear.
5.2.5 صحیح کام کے لباس کی مثال
5.2.5 Example of Correct Work Clothes
Correct work clothes should follow these points:
Wear work clothes without pockets or buttons.
Wear a hairnet inside the cap.
Use a standing collar so hair and dust do not easily attach.
Wear clean work clothes.
Put the ends of shirts and inner clothes inside the trousers.
Wear clean footwear.
Use sleeves and hems tightened with elastic.
Wear clothes without buttons, such as a T-shirt, under the work jacket.
When necessary, wear an apron and arm covers over the work clothes.
5.3 ہاتھ دھونا
5.3 Handwashing
Workers must wash their hands so that microorganisms on their hands and fingers do not contaminate raw materials or food.
Handwashing is necessary in the following situations:
Before entering the workplace
Immediately before starting work in the workplace
After using the toilet
After touching raw meat, fish, eggs, etc., and before touching other food or tools
Before doing work where food is touched directly, such as plating or arranging food
When moving from a contaminated work area to a non-contaminated / clean work area
5.3.1 ہاتھ دھونے کا صحیح طریقہ
5.3.1 Correct Handwashing Method
Step 1
Wash away dirt well with running water.
Step 2
Apply soap and wash the hands, fingers, and wrists.
Wash especially carefully:
Between the fingers
Fingertips
Under the nails
Wash for about 30 seconds.
Step 3
Rinse off the soap well with running water.
Rinse for about 20 seconds.
Step 4
Dry your hands using:
Disposable paper towel, or
Air towel / hand dryer
Step 5
Spray disinfecting alcohol on both hands and rub it well into both hands.
5.4 دستانے
5.4 Gloves
Even when wearing gloves, workers must not think that their hands are always clean.
Gloves can also become dirty or contaminated.
Workers must wash their hands before wearing gloves.
Gloves must be changed when necessary, for example:
When they become dirty
When they are torn
When changing work tasks
After touching raw meat, fish, eggs, or other contaminated materials
Before touching ready-to-eat food or cooked food
If contaminated gloves touch food, raw materials, machines, or tools, microorganisms may spread.
Therefore, gloves must be used correctly.
5.5 کام کی جگہ میں رویہ
5.5 Behavior in the Workplace
Workers must behave carefully inside the workplace to prevent contamination and foreign objects.
Important rules include:
Do not touch the face, hair, nose, mouth, or ears during work.
If you touch them, wash your hands again or change gloves.
Do not wipe your hands on work clothes.
Do not bring personal belongings into the workplace.
Do not bring documents with staples or clips into the workplace.
Do not bring pencils or mechanical pencils into the workplace.
Do not bring strong-smelling items such as perfume into the workplace.
Examples of personal items that should not be brought into the workplace include:
Watch
Smartphone
Wallet
Photos
Cigarettes
Staples, clips, pencil lead, and broken mechanical pencil lead can become foreign objects in food.
Smell is not a hazard, but if food absorbs a strong smell, the quality of the food becomes bad. Therefore, perfume and other strong-smelling items must not be brought into the workplace.
5.6 ٹوائلٹ کے اصول
5.6 Toilet Rules
When using the toilet, workers must follow the workplace rules carefully.
Important points are:
Change into footwear used only for the toilet.
Do not use workplace shoes inside the toilet.
Wash hands properly after using the toilet.
Before returning to the workplace, follow the handwashing and hygiene procedure again.
Toilet areas may contain microorganisms such as Norovirus. Therefore, handwashing after using the toilet is especially important.
Workers must not bring toilet contamination back into the food manufacturing area.
5.7 زوننگ اور کارکنوں کی نقل و حرکت
5.7 Zoning and Worker Movement
Workers must not move freely between dirty and clean areas.
When moving from a contaminated work area to a non-contaminated or clean work area, workers must wash hands and follow the required hygiene procedure.
Workers must not go outside the workplace while wearing the same work clothes, cap, or shoes used in the workplace.
If they go outside and return with the same clothes or shoes, they may bring the following into the workplace:
Soil
Dust
Insects
Microorganisms
Before entering the workplace, workers should remove hair and dust from their work clothes using an adhesive roller or air shower.
5.8 کام کے دوران ممنوعہ چیزیں
5.8 Prohibited Items During Work
Workers must not bring unnecessary items into the workplace because they may cause contamination or foreign objects.
Do not bring:
Watches
Smartphones
Wallets
Cigarettes
Photos
Accessories
Staples
Clips
Pencils
Mechanical pencils
Perfume or strong-smelling items
Workers must also avoid wearing:
Rings
Bracelets
Wristwatches
Earrings
Necklaces
Piercings
Hairpins
False eyelashes
Artificial nails
Nail polish
Nails must always be kept short.
Work clothes, masks, hairnets, caps, shoes, and gloves must be clean and must fit the worker’s size.
6. خام مال اور خوراک کی صفائی کا انتظام
6. Hygiene Management of Raw Materials and Food
To make safe and good-quality food, it is important to:
Use safe and good-quality raw materials
Manage the manufacturing method correctly
6.1 خام مال وصول کرنا اور محفوظ رکھنا
6.1 Receiving and Storing Raw Materials
6.1.1 خام مال وصول کرنا
6.1.1 Receiving Raw Materials
When receiving raw materials from outside, workers must compare the delivered raw materials with the order documents.
This check is called receiving inspection.
Workers must confirm:
Whether the correct raw materials have arrived
Appearance of raw materials
Smell
Standard / specification, such as size and quantity
Packaging condition
Expiry date and storage method display
Product temperature
Do not accept raw materials if:
They are spoiled
The packaging is broken
The expiry date has passed
The storage method was not proper
In such cases, report to the person in charge and return or exchange the materials.
If raw materials require receiving inspection, accept them only after confirming that they passed the inspection.
Raw materials that need refrigeration or freezing must not be left at room temperature for a long time.
6.1.2 خام مال محفوظ کرنا
6.1.2 Storing Raw Materials
The storage method depends on the type of raw material.
Each raw material must be stored at the decided temperature.
Main storage temperatures
Note 1: Grain processed products should be stored at room temperature in a dry place. Note 2: Some packed cut vegetables may have a specified storage temperature.
6.1.3 ریفریجریٹر اور فریزر کا استعمال
6.1.3 Using Refrigerators and Freezers
Do not pack raw materials tightly inside a refrigerator.
If there is no space, cold air cannot circulate throughout the refrigerator, and the raw materials cannot be cooled properly.
For freezers, it is better to fill frozen foods about 80% to 90% full. Frozen items cool each other, and this helps save electricity.
Do not leave refrigerator or freezer doors open because the low temperature cannot be maintained.
The temperature inside refrigerators and freezers must be measured regularly every day, and the results must be recorded.
Under the Food Sanitation Act:
Freezers must be -15°C or below
Refrigerators must be 10°C or below
Shelves and floors inside refrigerators and freezers must be cleaned regularly so that the inside stays clean.
6.2 استعمال کی آخری تاریخ، بہترین ذائقہ کی تاریخ، اور استعمال کی مدت
6.2 Use-By Date, Best-Before Date, and Expiry Date
6.2.1 استعمال کی آخری تاریخ
6.2.1 Use Limit
The use limit means the last day on which a raw material may be used.
Raw materials whose use limit has passed must not be used.
The use limit is decided for each raw material by the food manufacturing factory that uses that raw material.
6.2.2 بہترین ذائقہ کی تاریخ
6.2.2 Best-Before Date
If a raw material is a processed food packed in a container or bag, either a best-before date or an expiry date is shown on the package.
The best-before date is the last day when an unopened processed food can be eaten deliciously, if it has been stored under the decided conditions.
Even after the best-before date has passed, the food may still be edible. However, in food manufacturing factories, raw materials whose best-before date has passed are generally not used.
6.2.3 استعمال کی آخری محفوظ تاریخ
6.2.3 Expiry Date / Consumption Deadline
The expiry date is the last day when an unopened processed food can be eaten or used, if it has been stored under the decided conditions.
Food or raw materials whose expiry date has passed must never be used because the risk of food poisoning becomes high.
6.2.4 پہلے آیا، پہلے استعمال
6.2.4 First In, First Out
Raw materials delivered first should be used first.
This is called First In, First Out.
Usually, raw materials delivered earlier also have earlier best-before dates or expiry dates.
6.3 خام مال کھولنا اور ابتدائی تیاری
6.3 Opening and Pre-Processing Raw Materials
6.3.1 پیکنگ کھولنا
6.3.1 Opening Packages
When opening raw materials, workers must be careful that packaging materials do not accidentally enter the product.
Packaging materials include:
Cardboard boxes
Kraft paper
Plastic baskets for vegetables
Plastic bags used for frozen meat
To prevent pieces of opened bags or containers from mixing into food:
Do not cut the same place two or more times.
Confirm that the cut edge and the cut piece match.
Confirm that the number of opened bags or containers is the same as the number of cut pieces.
6.3.2 ڈی فروسٹنگ / پگھلانا
6.3.2 Thawing
There are several methods for thawing frozen raw materials:
Thawing at room temperature, called natural thawing
Thawing in a refrigerator, called refrigerator thawing
Thawing under running tap water, called running-water thawing
When thawing meat or fish, liquid may come out. This liquid is called drip.
To prevent drip from touching other foods or raw materials, thaw meat and fish in a tray.
Sometimes workers place the raw material on a mesh tray in a thawing rack so that the drip flows directly into the drain.
When using running-water thawing, check that the sealed bag has no holes or tears.
If the bag has holes or tears, part of the raw material may flow out into the running water.
If the bag is damaged, move the material into another bag, seal it, and then thaw it.
6.3.3 گرم کرکے نرم کرنا
6.3.3 Warming
Solid fats and hard paste-like raw materials can become easier to remove from their containers if warmed.
However, if they are warmed more than necessary, the quality of the raw material may become worse.
Therefore, raw materials that are warmed must be carefully controlled.
6.3.4 دھونا
6.3.4 Washing
Many raw materials are washed with water to remove foreign objects and dirt.
Seafood and seaweed may sometimes be washed with salt water of the proper concentration.
Raw materials for foods that do not have a heating process, such as vegetable salad, are disinfected using chemicals.
6.3.5 چھانٹنا، الگ کرنا، اور کاٹنا
6.3.5 Sorting, Separating, and Cutting
Raw materials that must not be used should be surely discarded.
Examples include:
Materials with dirt
Materials with unusual color
Materials with unusual hardness
Materials with unusual smell
Vegetable roots, sprouts, and skins that are removed
Fish scales and fins that are removed
These removed parts must not be used in food manufacturing.
When using raw meat, an X-ray foreign object detector may be used to check whether bones remain inside the meat.
If bones are found, that part must be removed.
6.4 مینوفیکچرنگ پروسیس میں صفائی کا انتظام
6.4 Hygiene Management During the Manufacturing Process
6.4.1 مینوفیکچرنگ مشینوں اور آلات کی دھلائی
6.4.1 Washing Manufacturing Equipment
Equipment used for food manufacturing, such as equipment for:
Cutting
Mixing
Heating
Forming / shaping
must be washed and kept clean.
If water remains inside manufacturing equipment or pipes connected to the equipment, that water may be contaminated with microorganisms.
In such cases:
Wash the manufacturing equipment again, or
Flush out the remaining water from the pipes using hot water or clean water.
If food containing allergens has been manufactured, the equipment used for manufacturing must be washed carefully so that allergens do not remain in the equipment.
Sometimes, foods containing allergens are manufactured at the end of the day, and then the equipment is washed carefully afterward.
6.4.2 حرارت سے جراثیم ختم کرنا
6.4.2 Heat Sterilization
Heating raw materials can make them:
Easier to eat
Easier to digest
More delicious
In addition, proper heating can remove biological hazards, such as pathogenic microorganisms, contained in the materials.
This is called heat sterilization.
Some bacteria are heat-resistant, such as spore-forming bacteria, and may not die even above 100°C.
Therefore, when killing microorganisms by heating, the heating temperature and heating time must be set correctly.
Spore-forming bacteria include:
Bacillus cereus
Clostridium perfringens
Clostridium botulinum
Examples of heating temperature and time to kill microorganisms
Heat sterilization must be controlled by checking the center temperature of the raw material or food.
When heating large raw materials, such as a large piece of meat, always confirm the center temperature.
6.4.3 حرارت کے بعد فوری ٹھنڈا کرنا
6.4.3 Cooling After Heat Sterilization
Many pathogenic microorganisms can be killed by heat sterilization.
However, some pathogenic microorganisms may not be completely killed. A small number may survive.
If cooked food cools slowly, surviving microorganisms may increase inside the food and cause food poisoning.
Therefore, after heating is finished, it is important to cool the food quickly.
The required cooling standards are:
Lower the center temperature to 20°C within 30 minutes after heating, or
Lower the center temperature to 10°C within 60 minutes after heating.
Cooling methods include:
Air cooling — cooling by blowing air
Water cooling — cooling with cold water
Quick cooling is also important to protect the taste and quality of the finished food.
6.4.4 کیمیکل کے ذریعے جراثیم ختم کرنا
6.4.4 Chemical Sterilization
For foods that are not heated, chemicals may be used to kill pathogenic microorganisms.
This is called chemical sterilization.
The concentration of the chemical and the treatment time are very important.
For example, when using sodium hypochlorite solution:
200 ppm for 5 minutes, or
100 ppm for 10 minutes
must be used as the treatment condition.
6.4.5 فریزنگ / منجمد کرنا
6.4.5 Freezing
Freezing means cooling fresh food or processed food until it freezes in order to improve its storage life.
A freezer is used to freeze food and store frozen food.
If food is frozen slowly, its texture, such as hardness and elasticity, and its flavor may become worse.
Therefore, to freeze food deliciously and maintain quality, it is necessary to freeze it quickly in a short time.
6.5 بھرائی، پیکنگ اور معائنہ
6.5 Filling, Packaging, and Inspection
Food in which hazards have been sufficiently reduced through sorting, sterilization, and other processes is filled into clean containers or packaged.
This is done to prevent hazards from entering from outside.
Containers and packaging materials used for filling and packaging must be clean, such as:
Bottles
Caps
Bags
Workers must not touch:
The mouth of bottles
Caps
The inside of bags
with their hands.
For processed foods to be shipped, the best-before date or expiry date must be printed correctly on the container or package.
Many mistakes happen when printing date numbers. Therefore, it is necessary to make careful checks, such as:
Another person checks that the printer setting is correct.
Another person checks that the printed content is correct.
After filling and packaging, products may be inspected to check:
Whether there are small holes in the container or package
Whether air is leaking out or entering
Whether the contents are leaking
6.5.1 میٹل ڈیٹیکٹر
6.5.1 Metal Detector
If there is a possibility that metal may enter food, the food may be inspected using a metal detector.
This is because metal, among hard foreign objects, can be found by a metal detector.
For managing a metal detector, the important point is to use a test piece before starting foreign-object inspection and confirm that the machine can detect metal correctly.
When manufacturing is finished, the test piece is used again to confirm that the metal detector worked normally until the end.
The size of the test piece used is decided by each food manufacturing factory.
If the metal detector does not work normally, the worker must immediately report it to the person in charge.
Food made between the time when the metal detector was confirmed to be working normally and the time when it was found not working normally may contain metal.
6.5.2 ایکس رے فارن آبجیکٹ ڈیٹیکٹر
6.5.2 X-Ray Foreign Object Detector
An X-ray foreign object detector can detect hard foreign objects that cannot be found by a metal detector, such as:
Stones
Glass
It may also be used for:
Receiving inspection of raw materials
Checking shortage in product quantity
Checking defective product shape
Plastic is difficult to detect with X-rays.
Therefore, workers must not rely only on the X-ray foreign object detector. They must prevent plastic from entering food from machines and equipment.
6.5.3 مائیکرو آرگنزم ٹیسٹ
6.5.3 Microorganism Inspection
For foods for which microorganism inspection is required by law, the decided items must be tested using the decided method.
In voluntary microorganism inspections carried out by food manufacturers, the following are generally tested:
General viable bacteria count
E. coli
Coliform bacteria
Other bacteria may also be tested, such as:
Staphylococcus aureus
Salmonella
Campylobacter
6.6 اسٹوریج، شپمنٹ اور ڈلیوری
6.6 Storage, Shipment, and Delivery
Food products that pass inspection are stored in the product warehouse until shipment.
Storage conditions, such as storage temperature, are decided for each food product.
6.6.1 پروڈکٹ سیمپل محفوظ کرنا
6.6.1 Storing Product Samples
If a complaint is received about a shipped product, it may be necessary to check whether products manufactured during the same period have the same problem.
For this reason, a certain percentage of samples are taken from the products and stored.
6.6.2 شپمنٹ اور ڈلیوری
6.6.2 Shipment and Delivery
Food products that entered the product warehouse first should be shipped first.
This is also called First In, First Out.
During transportation, food must be transported at the temperature decided for each food product so that its quality does not become worse.
7. HACCP کے ذریعے صفائی کا انتظام
7. Hygiene Management Through HACCP
HACCP is a system used to make sure that food is manufactured without hazards.
In HACCP, the workplace does the following:
Check what hazards may occur in each process.
Decide the most important processes, called Critical Control Points / CCPs.
Decide the control standards for those important processes.
Monitor whether the standards are being followed.
If the standards are not followed, do not ship that food product.
This system is called hygiene management through HACCP. Some foods may have more than one Critical Control Point.
7.1 HACCP کے سات اصول
7.1 Seven Principles of HACCP
HACCP is operated according to the following seven principles.
اصول 1: خطرے کا تجزیہ
Principle 1: Hazard Analysis
Check each manufacturing process and analyze what hazards may occur.
Hazards may include:
Physical hazards
Chemical hazards
Biological hazards
For example, workers must think:
Can metal pieces enter the food?
Can allergens remain in the machine?
Can bacteria increase during storage?
Can food poisoning bacteria survive if heating is not enough?
This checking and analysis is called hazard analysis.
اصول 2: اہم کنٹرول پوائنٹ کا تعین
Principle 2: Determination of Critical Control Points
If safe food cannot be manufactured by general hygiene management alone, the workplace decides the important process needed to remove or control the hazard.
This important process is called a Critical Control Point / CCP.
اصول 3: کنٹرول اسٹینڈرڈ مقرر کرنا
Principle 3: Setting Control Standards
For each Critical Control Point, the workplace decides the control standard.
A control standard means a clear condition that must be followed.
Examples:
Heat at 75°C for 1 minute or more
Cool to the decided temperature within the decided time
Use disinfectant at the decided concentration for the decided time
Confirm that the metal detector can detect the test piece
The control standard must be followed without fail.
اصول 4: مانیٹرنگ کا طریقہ مقرر کرنا
Principle 4: Setting the Monitoring Method
The workplace decides how to monitor the condition of each Critical Control Point.
Monitoring means checking whether the control standard is being followed.
Examples:
Measure the center temperature.
Check the heating time.
Check the disinfectant concentration.
Check the cooling time and temperature.
Check the metal detector with a test piece.
Write the result in the record sheet.
اصول 5: اصلاحی کارروائی مقرر کرنا
Principle 5: Setting Corrective Actions
If a Critical Control Point goes outside the control standard, the workplace must decide in advance what action to take.
For example, workers must decide:
How to return the process to the correct standard.
What to do with the food already made before the problem was found.
Whether the food can be reprocessed.
Whether the food must be stopped from shipment.
Who must be informed.
This action is called a corrective action.
اصول 6: تصدیق کا طریقہ مقرر کرنا
Principle 6: Setting the Verification Method
The workplace decides how to confirm that HACCP is working correctly.
Verification means checking whether the HACCP plan and monitoring are really effective.
Examples:
Check whether records are written correctly.
Check whether measuring equipment works correctly.
Check whether the monitoring method is being followed.
Check whether corrective actions were done properly.
اصول 7: ریکارڈ رکھنا
Principle 7: Record Keeping
The workplace records the results of HACCP management.
Records are important because they prove that hygiene management was done correctly.
Records may include:
Heating temperature
Heating time
Cooling temperature
Cooling time
Disinfectant concentration
Metal detector check result
X-ray detector check result
Corrective action record
Verification result
The results confirmed by the method decided in Principle 6 are also recorded.
7.2 اہم کنٹرول پوائنٹ اور کنٹرول اسٹینڈرڈ
7.2 Critical Control Points and Control Standards
Many foods need Critical Control Points.
The process that becomes a Critical Control Point depends on the type of food.
For example, in prepared foods, the Critical Control Point may be different depending on whether the food is heated or not heated.
At a Critical Control Point, the control standard must always be followed.
Typical processes that may become Critical Control Points are:
Heat sterilization
Cooling after heat sterilization
Sterilization by chemicals
Metal detector inspection
X-ray foreign object detector inspection
7.3 کنٹرول اسٹینڈرڈ سے انحراف
7.3 Deviation from Control Standards
When the decided control standard is not actually followed, it is called deviation from the control standard.
Examples:
The standard says to heat the center temperature at 75°C for 1 minute or more, but the food was actually heated only at 70°C for 1 minute.
The standard says to soak in 100 ppm disinfectant solution for 10 minutes, but the food was actually soaked only in 100 ppm disinfectant solution for 5 minutes.
In such cases, the control standard has not been followed, so the worker must report and take the decided corrective action.
7.4 اصلاحی کارروائی
7.4 Corrective Action
When a deviation happens, workers must not ignore it.
They must follow the workplace’s decided corrective action.
Corrective actions may include:
Stop the process.
Report to the person in charge.
Separate the affected food.
Reheat, recool, or reprocess the food if allowed.
Do not ship unsafe food.
Check why the deviation happened.
Prevent the same problem from happening again.
7.5 ریکارڈ
7.5 Records
Records must be written correctly and honestly.
Records help the company confirm:
The food was made safely.
The control standards were followed.
Problems were handled correctly.
Unsafe products were not shipped.
If there is a complaint or problem later, records help the company check what happened during manufacturing.
باب 3: کام کی جگہ کی حفاظت
Chapter 3: Occupational Safety
1. غذائی و مشروبات سازی کی صنعت میں کام کے حادثات
1. Workplace Accidents in the Food and Beverage Manufacturing Industry
In food manufacturing factories, workplace accidents can happen.
A workplace accident means an injury, sickness, or other damage that happens during work.
The purpose of occupational safety is to protect the safety and health of workers.
1.1 تجربہ اور کام کے حادثات کا تعلق
1.1 Relationship Between Experience and Workplace Accidents
About half of workplace accidents in food manufacturing happen to inexperienced workers.
There are many dangers in the workplace. Workers who have little experience and are not used to the work often cannot easily notice these dangers.
Because of this, inexperienced workers are more likely to have workplace accidents than workers who have worked for a long time.
1.2 اہم کام کے حادثات
1.2 Main Workplace Accidents
In food manufacturing factories, the most common workplace accidents are:
Falling / slipping / tripping
Being caught or巻き込まれ / pulled into machinery
Cuts and abrasions
These three types make up about 60% of all workplace accidents in food manufacturing factories.
1.2.1 گرنا، پھسلنا، یا ٹھوکر لگنا
1.2.1 Falling / Slipping / Tripping
This type of accident can happen when:
The floor is wet
Oil or food pieces are on the floor
A worker runs in the workplace
Objects are left in walkways
A worker is not looking carefully while walking
Workers may injure their legs, arms, head, or back.
1.2.2 مشین میں پھنس جانا یا کھنچ جانا
1.2.2 Being Caught or Pulled Into Machinery
This accident can happen when using moving machines.
A worker’s hand, finger, arm, clothing, or body may get caught in the machine.
This can cause very serious accidents. Fingers or bones may be crushed, and in large machines, a worker may even die.
1.2.3 کٹ لگنا یا رگڑ لگنا
1.2.3 Cuts and Abrasions
This accident can happen when using:
Knives
Cutters
Cutting machines
Slicers
It can also happen when food materials get stuck in a machine and a worker puts their hand inside.
The worker may cut their hand or even lose a finger.
1.2.4 اونچی جگہ سے گرنا
1.2.4 Falling from a Height
This can happen when:
Going down stairs
Working on a ladder
Working on a stepladder
Working in a high place
A worker may twist an ankle, hit the body, or break a bone.
1.2.5 جلنا
1.2.5 Burns
Burn accidents can happen when:
Using machines such as ovens
Handling hot water
Handling hot food
A worker may burn hands or feet. In serious cases, the face or whole body may be burned.
1.2.6 کمر درد
1.2.6 Back Pain
Back pain can happen when:
Lifting heavy things
Carrying heavy things repeatedly
Working in the same posture for a long time
Bending the back incorrectly
Workers must use correct posture and ask for help when something is heavy.
1.2.7 ہیٹ اسٹروک / گرمی سے بیماری
1.2.7 Heatstroke
Heatstroke can happen in hot workplaces.
Workers should be careful when:
The workplace temperature is high
Humidity is high
They sweat a lot
They do not drink enough water
Heatstroke can become serious, so workers must report immediately if they feel unwell.
1.2.8 ٹکراؤ
1.2.8 Collision
Collision accidents can happen when workers collide with:
Other workers
Carts
Moving equipment
Forklifts
If a worker is hit by a fast-moving forklift, it may cause death.
2. کام کے حادثات ختم کرنے کے لیے اہم باتیں
2. Important Things to Follow to Eliminate Workplace Accidents
Workers must follow the instructions of the person in charge and obey the decided rules.
They should also participate in workplace activities whose goal is to eliminate workplace accidents.
To eliminate workplace accidents, the following points are important:
5S
Correct clothing
Follow work procedure manuals and rules
Use machines correctly
Be careful when using detergents and chemicals
2.1 فائیو ایس / 5S
2.1 5S
Even when work is busy, it is important to keep the workplace in the same safe condition and pay attention to your own safety and the safety of people around you.
The 5S learned in Chapter 2 is also important for preventing workplace accidents.
5S means:
Seiri — Sorting Remove unnecessary things from the workplace.
Seiton — Setting in order Put necessary things in fixed places so workers can use them safely.
Seiso — Cleaning Clean the workplace and remove dirt, water, oil, and waste.
Seiketsu — Cleanliness Keep the workplace clean and safe at all times.
Shitsuke — Discipline / Habit Follow the decided rules every day.
2.2 درست لباس
2.2 Correct Clothing
Workers must wear the correct clothing and protective equipment for their work.
Examples include:
Work clothes
Safety shoes
Helmet
Gloves
Apron
Arm covers
Mask
Hairnet
Cap
Loose clothing, accessories, or items hanging from the body can get caught in machines.
Workers must wear clothes correctly and must not work in a careless style.
2.3 کام کے طریقہ کار اور اصولوں پر عمل کرنا
2.3 Follow Work Procedure Manuals and Rules
Workers must follow the decided work procedure manual and workplace rules.
They must not work by their own judgment if they are unsure.
If they do not understand something, they should ask the person in charge.
Ignoring the procedure can cause:
Injuries
Product defects
Machine trouble
Food safety problems
2.4 مشینوں کو صحیح طریقے سے استعمال کرنا
2.4 Use Machines Correctly
Machines must be used according to the decided method.
Important safety rules include:
Do not put hands into a moving machine.
Stop the machine before cleaning, checking, or removing stuck materials.
Do not remove safety covers.
Do not operate machines without permission or training.
Confirm that emergency stop buttons and safety devices work properly.
Report immediately if there is an abnormal sound, smell, vibration, or movement.
Machines can cause serious accidents if used incorrectly.
2.5 ڈٹرجنٹ اور کیمیکل استعمال کرتے وقت احتیاط
2.5 Be Careful When Using Detergents and Chemicals
Detergents, disinfectants, and other chemicals can cause injury if used incorrectly.
They may damage the eyes or skin and may cause burns.
Workers must:
Use the chemical according to the decided concentration.
Wear protective equipment such as gloves and goggles when necessary.
Never mix chemicals without permission.
Store chemicals in the decided place.
Make sure every chemical container has a label.
Report unknown or unlabeled chemicals to the person in charge.
3. غیر معمولی صورتحال یا کام کے حادثے پر ردعمل
3. Response When an Abnormal Situation or Workplace Accident Occurs
3.1 غیر معمولی صورتحال پیش آئے تو
3.1 When an Abnormal Situation Occurs
If something abnormal happens during work, workers must not decide by themselves.
Examples of abnormal situations include:
A machine makes a strange sound.
A machine moves differently from usual.
There is a strange smell.
Something is broken.
A foreign object is found.
A product or raw material looks different from usual.
A chemical container has no label.
Water, oil, or food is spilled on the floor.
In such cases, workers must:
Stop the work if necessary.
Report immediately to the person in charge.
Follow the instructions of the person in charge.
Do not restart work without permission.
Quick reporting can prevent accidents and food safety problems.
3.2 کام کا حادثہ پیش آئے تو
3.2 When a Workplace Accident Occurs
If a workplace accident occurs, workers must respond calmly and quickly.
Important actions include:
Stop the machine if it is safe to do so.
Call the person in charge immediately.
Help the injured person, but do not do dangerous actions.
Do not move the injured person unnecessarily if the injury may be serious.
Keep other workers away from the dangerous place.
Follow workplace emergency rules.
Report the accident honestly and correctly.
After the accident, the workplace must check:
Why the accident happened
What rule was not followed
What danger was not noticed
How to prevent the same accident from happening again
The purpose is not only to handle the accident, but also to prevent another accident.
4. خطرہ محسوس کرنے کی صلاحیت پیدا کرنا
4. Developing the Ability to Notice Danger
To prevent workplace accidents, workers must develop the ability to notice danger.
Even if the workplace has rules, accidents can still happen if workers do not notice dangerous situations.
Workers should learn danger through:
Studying examples of workplace accidents
Participating in near-miss activities
Participating in KYT danger prediction training
4.1 کام کے حادثات کی مثالوں سے سیکھنا
4.1 Learning from Examples of Workplace Accidents
Workers can prevent accidents by studying examples of accidents that happened in the past.
When studying an accident example, think about:
What happened?
Why did it happen?
What danger was present?
What should have been done?
Could the same thing happen in our workplace?
How can we prevent it?
Learning from other people’s accidents helps workers avoid repeating the same mistakes.
4.2 ہیاری ہٹ سرگرمی
4.2 Near-Miss Activity
A near-miss means a situation where a worker feels:
“That was dangerous!”
“I almost had an accident!”
“I was surprised and scared!”
In Japanese, this is called Hiyari-Hatto.
It is said that before one serious workplace accident happens, there may be:
29 minor injuries or small accidents
300 near-miss incidents with no injury
This is called Heinrich’s Law.
According to this idea, if near-miss incidents are reduced, serious workplace accidents are also less likely to happen.
4.2.1 ہیاری ہٹ سرگرمی کا مقصد
4.2.1 Purpose of Near-Miss Activity
Near-miss activity means:
Workers report near-miss experiences to each other.
These experiences are recorded.
Workers, supervisors, and managers share the information.
Everyone thinks about the cause of the near-miss.
Everyone discusses how to stop it from happening again.
The factory is improved based on these ideas.
The important point is to share as many near-miss experiences as possible.
Near-miss reports should not be hidden. They are useful for safety.
4.3 خطرے کی پیش گوئی کی تربیت
4.3 KYT — Danger Prediction Training
KYT means Kiken Yochi Training, or Danger Prediction Training.
In KYT, workers look at a work situation, picture, or actual workplace and think:
What dangers are hidden here?
What accident could happen?
Why could it happen?
What should we do to prevent it?
KYT helps workers notice danger before an accident happens.
4.3.1 KYT میں سوچنے کے اہم سوالات
4.3.1 Important Questions in KYT
During KYT, workers should think about these points:
What is dangerous? Identify unsafe places, machines, actions, or conditions.
What accident may happen? For example, slipping, falling, cutting, burning, getting caught in a machine, or collision.
What is the cause? For example, wet floor, wrong posture, machine not stopped, no protective equipment, or poor visibility.
What action should be taken? For example, clean the floor, stop the machine, wear gloves, use correct tools, or report to the person in charge.
4.3.2 KYT کا فائدہ
4.3.2 Benefits of KYT
KYT helps workers:
Notice danger early
Think before acting
Avoid careless work
Follow safety rules
Protect themselves and other workers
Reduce workplace accidents
Workers should participate actively in KYT and use what they learn in daily work.
5. اہم کام کے حادثات کی ہیاری ہٹ مثالیں اور حادثات کی روک تھام
5. Near-Miss Examples of Major Workplace Accidents and Accident Prevention
5.1 پھسلنا، ٹھوکر لگنا، گرنا
5.1 Falling / Slipping / Tripping Accidents
مثال 1
Example 1
صورتحال
Situation
A worker was carrying a tray with both hands and was walking to another place.
The worker slipped on a wet floor and almost fell.
وجہ
Cause
The water on the floor had not been wiped properly.
احتیاطی تدبیر
Preventive Measure
If the floor is wet, wipe the water properly.
Do not leave water on the floor.
مثال 2
Example 2
صورتحال
Situation
A worker was carrying boxes of bread with both hands and walking through the parking area toward a truck.
The worker tripped over an empty box left in the passage and almost fell.
وجہ
Cause
An empty box was left in the passage.
The worker was carrying stacked bread boxes, so it was difficult to see the feet.
Safety check of the passage was not enough.
احتیاطی تدبیر
Preventive Measure
Do not leave things in the passage, even if they are used for work.
Before starting work, check that passages are safe.
When carrying stacked boxes, carry only the number of boxes that allows you to see the front clearly.
5.2 مشین میں پھنسنا یا کھنچ جانا
5.2 Being Caught or Pulled Into Machinery
مثال 1
Example 1
صورتحال
Situation
A machine on the packaging line stopped because a product was stuck.
Without pressing the stop button, the worker opened the cover and tried to remove the stuck product.
Then the machine suddenly started moving, and the worker’s hand was almost caught.
وجہ
Cause
The worker did not press the stop button and did not confirm that the packaging line had completely stopped.
The machine did not have a safety device that automatically stops the machine when the cover is opened.
احتیاطی تدبیر
Preventive Measure
Install a safety device that automatically stops the machine when the cover is opened.
Before doing any work, press the stop button and confirm that the machine has stopped completely.
Start work only after confirming that the machine is stopped.
مثال 2
Example 2
صورتحال
Situation
A worker was cleaning a belt conveyor without stopping it.
A cleaning cloth got caught, and the worker’s hand was almost pulled into the machine.
وجہ
Cause
The worker did not stop the belt conveyor before cleaning.
احتیاطی تدبیر
Preventive Measure
When doing work such as cleaning, removing foreign objects, inspection, or repair:
Press the stop button.
Confirm that the machine has surely stopped.
Start the work only after confirming safety.
5.3 کٹ لگنا یا رگڑ لگنا
5.3 Cuts and Abrasions
مثال 1
Example 1
صورتحال
Situation
A worker was slicing bread on a worktable while pushing the bread by hand.
The worker’s finger was almost cut by the blade of the slicer.
وجہ
Cause
The worker was slicing while pushing the bread by hand without using a push plate.
احتیاطی تدبیر
Preventive Measure
When using a slicer, use a push plate to push the bread forward.
Do not push food toward the blade with your hand.
مثال 2
Example 2
صورتحال
Situation
A worker was cutting frozen fish with a knife.
The knife held in the right hand slipped and almost hit the left hand, which could have caused a cut.
وجہ
Cause
The worker was not wearing cut-resistant protective gloves on the left hand.
احتیاطی تدبیر
Preventive Measure
Use a safe working posture.
Hold and cut the frozen fish in a way suitable for the work.
Wear cut-resistant gloves.
5.4 اونچی جگہ سے گرنا
5.4 Falling from a Height
مثال
Example
صورتحال
Situation
A worker took a product from a shelf and tried to climb down from a stepladder while holding the product.
The stepladder moved, the worker lost balance, and almost fell.
وجہ
Cause
The stepladder’s opening stopper was not set.
There was no person holding the stepladder to prevent it from falling.
احتیاطی تدبیر
Preventive Measure
Always set the opening stopper before using a stepladder.
When climbing up or down, keep one hand free and hold the stepladder.
If the product is large, work with two people.
5.5 جلنا
5.5 Burns
مثال
Example
صورتحال
Situation
A worker tried to move an oven iron plate to another worktable.
The worker did not realize that the iron plate was hot and almost touched it with bare hands.
وجہ
Cause
The iron plate had just been taken out of the oven and was very hot, but the worker tried to carry it with bare hands without checking its temperature.
احتیاطی تدبیر
Preventive Measure
When carrying iron plates, pots, kettles, or similar hot items, wear protective gloves.
Before carrying, check the temperature.
If the item is hot, warn people around you to be careful.
5.6 کمر درد
5.6 Back Pain
مثال
Example
صورتحال
Situation
A worker lifted a heavy kettle with both hands and felt strong pain in the lower back.
وجہ
Cause
The worker tried to lift a heavy object while keeping an unstable posture.
احتیاطی تدبیر
Preventive Measure
When lifting a heavy object:
Bring your body close to the heavy object.
Bend your knees.
Lower your waist.
Hold the heavy object close to your body.
Stand up slowly while straightening your knees.
When carrying heavy objects, use a cart as much as possible.
5.7 ہیٹ اسٹروک / گرمی سے بیماری
5.7 Heatstroke
مثال
Example
صورتحال
Situation
In the raw bean-paste process, a worker repeatedly put beans into a hopper and did weighing work.
While moving, the worker became unsteady and was diagnosed with heatstroke.
وجہ
Cause
The worker continued working in a place with high temperature and high humidity without taking breaks.
The worker sweated a lot but did not take enough water and salt.
احتیاطی تدبیر
Preventive Measure
When working in a place with high temperature and high humidity:
Take breaks often.
Drink water.
Take salt.
Wear clothes that allow air to pass through, absorb sweat and moisture, and dry easily.
اگر کام کی جگہ پر ہیٹ اسٹروک جیسی علامات ظاہر ہوں
If heatstroke-like symptoms appear at the workplace
Take the following action:
Inform the person in charge.
Move to a cool place.
Rest quietly.
Loosen the work clothes and cool the body, especially the neck, armpits, and groin area.
Drink water containing salt or a sports drink.
5.8 ٹکراؤ
5.8 Collision Accidents
مثال
Example
صورتحال
Situation
A forklift was carrying empty boxes to a storage area outside the workplace.
When the forklift passed the warehouse entrance without stopping temporarily, it almost collided with a person coming from the side.
وجہ
Cause
The forklift did not stop temporarily in front of the warehouse entrance.
احتیاطی تدبیر
Preventive Measure
The forklift driver must:
Stop temporarily in front of the warehouse entrance.
Point and call out to confirm safety.
Always check safety before moving forward.
The source text also notes that the organization’s website has study videos for preventing workplace accidents, accessible through the QR code in the original file.
اضافی مواد
Supplementary Materials
1. کام کی جگہ پر استعمال ہونے والے حفاظتی نشانات کی مثالیں
1. Examples of Safety Signs Used in Workplaces
The text gives examples of common safety signs used in food manufacturing workplaces. These signs help workers notice danger, follow rules, and respond quickly in emergencies.
1.1 داخلہ منع ہے
1.1 Entry Prohibited Sign
This sign means that workers must not enter that area.
It is used for places where entry is dangerous or restricted.
Workers must not enter unless they have permission from the person in charge.
1.2 دوڑنا منع ہے
1.2 No Running Sign
This sign means running is prohibited.
Running in the workplace can cause:
Slipping
Falling
Collision with other workers
Collision with machines or carts
Workers must walk carefully.
1.3 دروازہ کھلا چھوڑنا منع ہے
1.3 Do Not Leave the Door Open Sign
This sign means the door must not be left open.
Leaving doors open may allow the following to enter the workplace:
Dust
Insects
Rats or pests
Microorganisms
Outside air
Workers must close doors properly after passing through.
1.4 ہاتھ سے چھونا منع ہے
1.4 Do Not Touch by Hand Sign
This sign means workers must not touch that item or area with their hands.
It may be used where touching can cause:
Injury
Burns
Contamination
Machine trouble
Always follow the instruction shown by the sign.
1.5 پھنسنے کا خطرہ
1.5 Danger of Being Caught / Pinched Sign
This sign warns that hands, fingers, clothes, or body parts may be caught or pinched.
It is often used near machines with moving parts.
Workers must keep hands and clothes away from moving parts.
1.6 جلنے کا خطرہ
1.6 Burn Hazard Sign
This sign warns that there is a danger of burns.
It may be used near:
Ovens
Hot plates
Hot water
Steam
Heated machines
Workers must wear protective equipment when necessary and check the temperature before touching.
1.7 گرنے / پھسلنے کا خطرہ
1.7 Danger of Falling / Slipping Sign
This sign warns that there is a risk of falling, slipping, or tripping.
It may be used where:
The floor is wet
Oil or food pieces may be on the floor
Steps or uneven surfaces exist
Workers must walk carefully and keep the floor clean and dry.
1.8 اونچائی سے گرنے کا خطرہ
1.8 Danger of Falling from a Height Sign
This sign warns that there is a danger of falling from a high place.
It may be used near:
Ladders
Stepladders
Platforms
High shelves
Workers must use correct posture and safety rules when working at height.
1.9 کٹنے کا خطرہ
1.9 Cutting Hazard Sign
This sign warns that there is a danger of being cut.
It may be used near:
Slicers
Cutters
Knives
Cutting machines
Workers must use protective equipment and never put hands near blades carelessly.
1.10 مشین میں کھنچ جانے کا خطرہ
1.10 Danger of Being Pulled into Machinery Sign
This sign warns that clothes, hands, fingers, or tools may be pulled into a machine.
It may be used near:
Conveyors
Mixers
Rollers
Rotating machines
Before cleaning, checking, or removing stuck materials, stop the machine and confirm that it has completely stopped.
1.11 بجلی کا جھٹکا لگنے کا خطرہ
1.11 Electric Shock Hazard Sign
This sign warns of danger from electricity.
Workers must not touch electrical equipment with wet hands.
Only authorized or trained people should handle electrical equipment.
1.12 سر ٹکرانے کا خطرہ
1.12 Head Collision Hazard Sign
This sign warns that workers may hit their head.
It may be used in places with:
Low ceilings
Low pipes
Low beams
Low machine parts
Workers must watch their head and wear a helmet if required.
1.13 آنکھوں کی حفاظت ضروری ہے
1.13 Eye Protection Required Sign
This sign means workers must protect their eyes.
It may be used when there is a risk of:
Chemicals splashing
Dust entering the eyes
Small pieces flying
Cleaning agents or disinfectants splashing
Workers must wear safety goggles or other eye protection.
1.14 ایمرجنسی ایگزٹ / ہنگامی راستہ
1.14 Emergency Exit Sign
This sign shows the location of an emergency exit used for evacuation.
Workers must remember where emergency exits are located.
Emergency exits and evacuation routes must not be blocked by boxes, tools, or products.
1.15 آگ بجھانے والے آلے کی جگہ
1.15 Fire Extinguisher Location Sign
This sign shows where the fire extinguisher is placed.
Workers should know the location of fire extinguishers in the workplace.
Items must not be placed in front of fire extinguishers because they must be easy to use during an emergency.
2. کام کے حادثات روکنے کے لیے تعلیمی ویڈیوز
2. Study Videos for Preventing Workplace Accidents
The text also provides a list of study video links for preventing workplace accidents.
The videos are about major near-miss examples and accident prevention, such as:
Falling / slipping / tripping
Being caught or pulled into machinery
Cuts and abrasions
Falling objects
Burns
Back pain and foot injuries
The videos are available in several languages, including Japanese, English, Chinese, Vietnamese, Khmer, Myanmar language, Thai, Indonesian, and Nepali.
To watch the videos, the original text says to click the QR code or scan it with a smartphone.

いん

いん

りょう

の基

本

」と「労

（１） 食
しょく
品
ひん
衛
えい
生
せい
　「衛
えい
生
せい
」とはいのち（生
せい
命
めい
）をまもる（衛
まも
る）ことです。 「食
しょく
品
ひん
衛
えい
生
せい
」とは、
食
しょく
品
ひん
を食
た
べて、人
ひと
が病
びょう
気
き
にならないこと、ケガをしないことです。
　食
た
べた人
ひと
が病
びょう
気
き
になったりケガをしないように 食
しょく
品
ひん
を作
つく
る（管
かん
理
り
する）こ
とが、 食
しょく
品
ひん
製
せい
造
ぞう
の「衛
えい
生
せい
管
かん
理
り
」です。
　 食
しょく
品
ひん
の衛
えい
生
せい
管
かん
理
り
が 十
じゅう
分
ぶん
ではない 食
しょく
品
ひん
( 安
あん
全
ぜん
ではない 食
しょく
品
ひん
)を 食
た
べた人
ひと
は、 病
びょう
気
き
になったり、ケガをします。その 食
しょく
品
ひん
を作
つく
った会
かい
社
しゃ
は、信
しん
用
よう
をなく
します。
（２）労
ろう
働
どう
安
あん
全
ぜん
　食
しょく
品
ひん
製
せい
造
ぞう
工
こう
場
じょう
では、ケガなどの労
ろう
働
どう
災
さい
害
がい
が起
お
きています。
　工
こう
場
じょう
や作
さ

」です。

　「食

（１）５Ｓ
エス
　「 ５Ｓ
エス
」とは、 「Ｓ
エス
」で始

（※）を意

（※） 　…「整
せい
理
り
（Seiri） 」 「整
せい
頓
とん
（Seiton） 」 「清
せい
掃
そう
（Seisou） 」 「清
せい
潔
けつ
（Seiketsu） 」…
「習
しゅう
慣
かん
付
づ
け（Shu
ー
kanzuke）またはしつけ（Shitsuke） 」 　
　①……整
せい
理
り
（Seiri） 。使
つか
わないものをなくして、使
つか
うものだけにすること。い
らない器
き
具
ぐ
や道
どう
具
ぐ
をなくして、必
ひつ
要
よう
な器
き
具

や道
どう
具

だけにすること。
　②……整
せい
頓
とん
（Seiton） 。器
き
具
ぐ
や道
どう
具
ぐ
を、必
ひつ
要
よう
なときにすぐに取
と
り出
だ
せるように
すること。そのために、場
ば
所
しょ
を決
き
めて置
お
いたり、使
つか
いやすい 順
じゅん
序
じょ
に並
なら
べ
たり、名
な
前
まえ
や数
すう
量
りょう
を表
ひょう
示
じ
したりすること。
　③……清
せい
掃
そう
（Seisou） 。設
せつ
備
び
や器
き
具
ぐ
、道
どう
具
ぐ
を、洗
あら
ったり、磨
みが
いたり、拭
ふ
いたり
してきれいな 状
じょう
態
たい
にすること。汚
よご
れを取
と
り除
のぞ
くこと。
　④……清
せい
潔
けつ
（Seiketsu） 。整
せい
理
り
、整
せい
頓
とん
、清
せい
掃
そう
をして見
み
た目

がきれいで、 食
しょく
品
ひん
衛
えい
生
せい
にも問
もん
題
だい
がないこと。設
せつ
備
び
や器
き
具
ぐ
が、 病
びょう
原
げん
性
せい
微
び
生
せい
物
ぶつ
に汚
お
染
せん
されていな
いこと。
　⑤……習
しゅう
慣
かん
付
づ
け（Shu
ー

き
められたこと
を決
き

せい

り
、整
せい

とん
、清
せい
掃
そう
、清
せい

けつ
のルールを
そのとおりに実
じっ
行
こう
できるようになること。
　施
し
設
せつ
、設
せつ
備
び
、器
き
具
ぐ
などを清
せい
潔
けつ

さ

や作
さ

微
び
生
せい

ぶつ

ち込
こ

げん

や食

に病
